PART IV. * NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended December 31, 1
CHINA SEA. No. 277. TIENTSIN DISTRICT. NoTinE is hereby given that Captain WILIAx P. CHAnD, of the Chinese s.s. Peiping, eports that he has repeatedly, on different voyages, obtained soundings in from 7 to 9 fathoms, low water, hard mud, about 14 miles to the N. 87j* E. (true) from Shaluitien Island (Tsao-fei-tien), where the latest Admiralty charts show 15 fathoms, and that he estimates the extent of the bank to be about 3 miles in an E.N.E. and W.S.W. direction, with a breadth of from I to Ij miles. On a straig t line from thip bank to a point bearing south 3 miles from Tsao-fei-tien Lighthouse, Captain CHA obtaine&amp;amp; no soundings of less depth than those indicated by the charts. SHANGHAI, 5tk October 1893. No. 278. CHEFOO DISTRICT. NORTH-EAST SHANTUNG PROMONTORY LIGHTHOUSE. EsTABLISHMENT OF FoG SIREN. NoTicE is hereby given that a first-class siren has been established at this station, and that during foggy or thick weather it will give 2 blasts, each of about two and a half iseconds duration (high note, low note), in quick succession every two minUtes. The former fog signal of this station, which was 2 guns fired with an interval of one mainute between them and repeated after an interval of io minutes if a vessel&#39;s signal continued to be heard, will not be given in future unless the siren be disabled. SHANGHAI, 6th November 1893.
No. 279. NEWCHWANG DISTRICT. DISCONTINUANCE OF LAO-T&#39;IEH-SIIAN PROMONTORY LIGHT DURING WINTER. NOTICE is hereby given that the exhibition of the above-named light will be discontinue each year on the 16th December and resumed on the iat March following. SHANGHAI, 9th November 1893. No. 280. YANGTZE RIVER--KIUKIANG DISTRICT. POINT OTTER, LAY ISIAND, BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the -Point Otter, Lay Island, Beacon Light has been shifte 50 yards S. 52&#39; W. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the rive bank. SHANGHAI, 23rd December 1893. No. 281. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. NORTH CHANNEL ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. CHANGES IN POSITIONS OF BUOYS. NoTICE is hereby given that a recent examination of the banks between Drinkwater Poin and the western end of Bush Island having shown that considerable alterations have take place, the buoys have been shifted was requisite in order to mark the channel to the bes advantage, and that their present positions are as follows:- Ch&#39;i-yao Bank Buoy.-A six-foot conical red buoy, surmounted by a black spherical cag moored off the south-eastern elbow of Ch&#39;i-yao Bank, to mark the starboard side of the channe entering, with Drinkwater Point yurveving Reacon bearing N.281o E., true, distant 2miles.
166 &#39;CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [OCT.-DEC., 1893.1 Middle Island Buoy.-A six-foot conical black buoy, surmounted by a black inverted frustum cage, moored off the northern elbow of the banks which lie to the northward of Middle and Blockhouse Islands, to mark the port side of the channel, entering, with the Blockhouse Island Surveying Beacon bearing S. 121( E., true, distant 61 miles. Tsngming Bank Buoy.-A six-foot conical red buoy, surmounted by a black spherical cage, moored off the south-eastern elbow of the Tsungming Bank, to mark the starboard side of the channel, entering, with the &quot; High Dark Tree&quot; on the south-western shore of Tsungming bearing N. 440o E., true, distant 4 miles. Note.-The northern shore of Bush Island is washing away, and the Tsungming Bank is extending to the southward and eastward. Vessels entering should, on passing the Tsungming Bank Buoy, steer for the western end of Bush Island and round it at about 2 cables. SHANGHAI, 23rd December 1893.

192 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. OCT.-DEC., (Beceived i 5th January I 894.). CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, STH JANUARY 1894. I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular NO. 381, Second Series, I now transmit o the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring tations in Native vessels for the fourth quarter of the year 1893, October-December. b. The Custom House (I . 1 ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin nd Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hklqs 3o and Hk.M 80 per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the ates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present otrolled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (t * ), Changchow (F c ), Fotochow ), and Kowloon (f I) stations. c. 1893.] LAPPA. (Received 15th January 1894-U M CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 9TH JANUARY 1894. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 38r, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the fourth quarter of the year 1893, October-December. b. The Custom House (A C ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (17k.IM 30 and Hk. M 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow ( j N f) and Chiensban (j Lh) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (; 1), Kuttai ( &#39;), and Kwanchiap (I $) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
